Code-split into its own chunk — `vendor-three` is not pulled into the admin bundle unless a GLB card actually renders.
- **Assigned/unassigned tagging.** `SiteAssetsEditor` derives a flat `assignedIds` set from `AssetConfig` (general + all drops + all storefront pages) and passes it down; cards show an **Assigned/Unassigned** badge and `MediaLibraryPage` gets a new "Assignment" filter (all/assigned/unassigned) alongside a new **Format** dropdown populated from the distinct mime types actually present in the library (`listPresentMediaFormats`). `filterMediaAssets` took an optional 4th `options` param (`formatFilter`, `assignmentFilter`, `assignedIds`) — existing 3-arg call sites untouched.
- **Coming Soon headline flash — actually fixed this time.** The prior fix (2026-07-12 entry below) gated the anti-flash CSS behind `@media (scripting: enabled)`, which is **Firefox-only** — Chrome/Safari/Edge never match it, so the pre-hide silently never applied for the vast majority of visitors and the SSR-painted, fallback-font, tight-`leading-[0.96]` headline (letters stacked on top of each other) flashed on every load before hydration. The rule is now unconditional; a `<noscript>` override in `ComingSoonExperience` restores visibility for the no-JS case the media query used to handle.
- **Verified**: `pnpm typecheck` clean; full suite 110 files / 610 tests green; `pnpm build` clean (`GlbAssetPreview` confirmed as its own small lazy chunk, separate from `vendor-three`).

## 2026-07-12 — Hero forge: particle form now matches the render's exact size

- **Fixed the particle silhouette rendering larger than the revealed product.** `sampleImageSilhouette` normalized the garment's **tight pixel bounds** to the fit, while the DOM reveal `object-contain`s the **full image box** (transparent padding included) — so any padding in the upload made the render smaller than the ember form. The sampler now maps points in **image-box coordinates** (largest image dim → fit, box centre → origin, no tight-bounds normalize), so particle form and rendered pixels match in size *and* position for any padding. Also aligned the particles' hover zoom (1.06 → 1.035) with the render's hover scale. Verified in-browser with a deliberately padded synthetic image: particle extents match the expected image-box mapping to 3 decimals (old behaviour stretched the same garment 2.5× in that test); `pnpm typecheck` + landing/coming-soon suites + build green.

## 2026-07-12 — Hero forge reveal = fusion; Coming Soon entrance gated on webfonts

- **Hero forge: the reveal now reads as the particles *becoming* the product.** Feedback on v2: the split/rearrange motion was right, but the render appeared beside the embers instead of out of them. Changes: `REVEAL_LEAD` 0.45s → 0.1s (`heroForgeTiming.ts`) so the embers are seen fully forming the silhouette before anything resolves; the shader's reveal behavior flipped from "outward halo breathe" to **condense** — the slab flattens onto the image plane (`pos.z ×(1−uReveal·0.85)`), shimmer stills (wobble ×(1−uReveal·0.9)), sparks shrink (point size ×(1−uReveal·0.35)) and alpha drops to a whisper (×(1−uReveal·0.94)) as the render materializes **in place** (DOM reveal de-theatricalized: scale 1.015/blur 6px from-state instead of 1.06/14px; strike-out now grows 1.012 + heats — disintegration, not shrink-fade). The heat-pulse flash at the crossover stays.
- **Coming Soon: cramped-letters flash on load fixed at the root — the entrance now waits for webfonts.** The `.cs-anim-pending` pre-hide (previous fix) covered the pre-hydration paint, but the GSAP entrance could still start before **Anton** loaded: the headline's `leading-[0.96]` is tuned for Anton, so the fallback font renders its lines overlapping ("letters cramped on top of each other") and SplitText measured the wrong glyph boxes. `useComingSoonEntrance` now builds the split + timeline (both branches) only after `document.fonts.ready` (capped at 1.5s so a stalled font API can never hold the page blank; jsdom/no-API environments resolve immediately), registered via `ctx.add()` so `matchMedia.revert()` still cleans up async-created tweens; the pre-hide stays on until then. Nothing paints until the real font is in and the timeline owns visibility.
- **Verified**: `pnpm verify` green (typecheck; 610 tests incl. all coming-soon + landing suites; build); reworked shaders compile clean on WebGL2.

## 2026-07-12 — Hero forge v2: particles form the product, the real render resolves in (2D silhouettes replace GLBs)

- **The hero forge now reveals the actual product, not a particle likeness.** The Higgsfield image-to-3D GLBs proved too distorted for garment fidelity, so the pipeline switched to the Coming Soon emblem technique: the CMS slots are now **`heroProductImage1..3`** (`kind: 'image'`, one front view on a **transparent background**; replaces `heroProductModel1..3` — never shipped/assigned, no migration needed) and each render's silhouette is **pixel-sampled** by the new shared `sampleImageSilhouette()` (`src/shared/webgl/particleShapes.ts`): alpha-gated sampling capped at 300px raster, flat normalize to fit, **fixed post-scale z-thickness** (±0.24 — emblometrically crisp, never ballooned), plus a per-point **luminance shade channel** normalized to the image's brightest pixel. CORS-tainted/broken uploads degrade to a disc. Because the particle targets are sampled from the *same pixels* as the revealed render, ember form and image are registered 1:1.
- **Reveal choreography** (`motion/heroForgeTiming.ts` is the shared clock for both trees): entry nebula → lineup of every piece → converge into piece 01 — then the **actual render resolves in** (fade + unblur + brightness cool-down, `FORGE_REVEAL_DURATION` 0.9s starting 0.45s before the morph settles) while the embers flash (`sin(uReveal·π)` heat pulse) and **recede to a faint halo** (alpha ×0.18, slight outward breathe, sway stilled). Each strike runs the reverse: render disintegrates (0.32s in-blur), the cloud re-forms the next silhouette (bright pixels — crest, piping — burn hotter via the shade channel, so even the ember form reads as *that* product), and the next render resolves. The DOM `<img>` sits in a 64.6svh square centred 26vw from the right edge = exactly `PIECE_FIT` (2.35) of the 3.64-unit world viewport, so pixels and particles stay registered. New `motion.heroProductReveal` crosses the reveal state DOM→WebGL; scrolling cancels it early (0.12–0.5 scrub) so the render dissolves back into embers and the embers do the creed hand-off (`[data-hero-product-render]` fade in `buildOathHero`); strikes are disarmed until the first reveal and past 65% of the pin. Hover: existing particle zoom + a 1.035 scale breathe on the render (CSS transition on a wrapper, no GSAP conflict). `HeroProductParticles` no longer touches `useGLTF`; targets load async and the forge mounts when sampled.
- **Verified**: `pnpm verify` green (110 files / 610 tests); home renders with zero console errors; `sampleImageSilhouette` exercised in-browser against a synthesized transparent product PNG (0 NaN, fit ±1.175, z exactly ±0.24, shade channel separates crest from fabric ~313/5687 pts) and a 404 URL (clean disc fallback); both new shaders compile clean on WebGL2. Docs: `cms-architecture.md` slot section updated. Awaiting real transparent product PNGs assigned in `/admin/assets` for the final visual pass.

## 2026-07-12 — Hero product forge (particle GLB showcase, superseded same day by v2 above) + Graphite & Champagne theme consolidation

- **New hero mode `products` — the CMS-assigned product GLBs forged out of ember particles on the hero stage**, replacing the video approach. New asset slots (`theOathAssetSlots.ts`): `heroMediaMode` gains `products` (now first), plus `heroProductModel1..3` (`kind: 'model'`, GLB < 4 MB each). Resolution (`theOathAssets.ts`): explicit mode wins; otherwise assigned models imply `products`; else the legacy video/image inference. Choreography (`webgl/HeroProductParticles.tsx`, 14k particles on the persistent OathCanvas): scattered nebula → **lineup of every assigned piece** (round-robin particle partition, each at 0.52× fit) → hold → converge into piece 01 full-size; each stage click dissolves and re-forges the next model (per-seed staggered morph + spark puff + burst heat); hover breathes a gentle zoom (`uZoom`); the hero scrub loosens/dims the cloud (`uScroll` ← `motion.heroProgress`) timed to the DOM film fade. Shaders in `webgl/heroForgeShaders.ts` (same engine family as the Coming Soon anvil; sampling now shared via `src/shared/webgl/particleShapes.ts`, which `comingSoonShapes.ts` re-exports).
- **Interaction lives in the DOM** (the canvas is pointer-transparent): new `OathHeroProductStage.tsx` — a full-panel keyboard-reachable strike button over the right stage + an active-piece plate (numeral / name / role / "Strike to re-forge") that crossfades per re-forge (`.oath-hero-piece-label` keyframes in `styles.css`); writes `heroProductIndex`/`heroProductHover`/`heroProductStrike` onto the extended `OathMotionState` bridge, which the WebGL reads in `useFrame`. Strikes are ignored past 65% of the pin (the stage has visually faded — added to the `buildOathHero` film-fade selector). Mobile / tablet / reduced-motion / no-WebGL get a static stand-in (poster → hero image → piece 01 render → themed duotone + crest) that hands off (fades) when `data-webgl="on"`.
- **Theme library consolidated to one house preset: `graphite-champagne` (Graphite & Champagne)** — near-black graphite surfaces (`#0A0B0C`/`#15171A`), bone type, muted champagne primary `#C4AE86`, champagne-bronze accent `#8E7A57`; foregrounds intentionally omitted so `finalizeThemePalette`/`bestForeground` guarantee the WCAG gate. All 12 exploration presets + the 2 legacy defaults are **retired**: `RETIRED_THEME_IDS` in `themeLibrary.ts` drops stored copies on parse and remaps a retired `activeThemeId` to the house preset (user-created `theme-<ts>` themes survive; the legacy single-config parse branch now maps to retired ids so pre-consolidation palettes migrate forward instead of resurrecting under the new id). `DEFAULT_THEME_PRESET_ID` → `graphite-champagne`; experience registry's advisory `recommendedThemeKey` updated; editor copy updated; `techForgePreset.test.ts` deleted; preset/publication/settings tests rewritten for the consolidation semantics.
- **Verified**: `pnpm verify` green (110 files / 610 tests). Live (dev, `?anvl-preview=live` — the published CMS currently has Coming Soon enabled): Graphite & Champagne is the live theme (`--color-bg #0A0B0C`, `--color-primary #C4AE86`); products-mode stage renders, gates on `data-webgl="on"`, and click-cycles the piece plate (01 ⇄ 02 with names); hero forge shaders compile clean on WebGL2; shared sampler on the real `anvil.glb` → 0 NaN, correctly fit. The R3F visual loop itself can't run in the hidden preview tab (rAF suspended — known environment limitation, see the perf audit) — worth one manual eyeball on a real browser with GLBs assigned.

## 2026-07-12 — Storefront entry −50 kB gzip (supabase-js deferred) + Coming Soon polish

- **Perf: `@supabase/supabase-js` removed from the every-route entry chunk.** The audit (`docs/audits/anvl-performance-audit.md`) had traced GoTrue + RealtimeClient into the always-loaded `index` chunk via a single eager import — `runtime.ts` statically pulled `supabaseAccountClient` (→ `storefrontSupabaseClient` → `createAnvlSupabaseClient` → supabase-js) into the `runtimeClients` singleton, even though account features only run on the `/account` + `/auth/*` routes. New `lazySupabaseAccountClient` keeps the `AccountClient` contract but dynamic-imports the real client on first method call; `runtime.ts` wires that instead. Result: **client entry `index` 930 kB → 733 kB raw / 283 kB → 233 kB gzip (−50 kB gzip on every page load)**; supabase-js now lives in its own `vendor-supabase` chunk (196 kB / 50 kB gzip, added to `manualChunks`) loaded only on auth/account routes. `pnpm verify` green (686 tests). All other heavy vendors (`three`, `gsap`, `framer-motion`, `lenis`, `fuse`) were already confirmed lazy/code-split — no change.
- **Coming Soon: text no longer flashes before the entrance animation.** The reveal page is SSR'd, so the browser painted the headline + copy once (un-animated, fallback font, letters cramped) before hydration ran the GSAP entrance. Added a `.cs-anim-pending` class on the reveal root (server-rendered) + a `@media (scripting: enabled)` CSS rule that pre-hides `[data-cs-reveal]/[data-cs-headline]/[data-cs-rule]`; `useComingSoonEntrance` drops the class only after applying its GSAP `from` states, so content now appears *while* it animates, never before. Gated to `scripting: enabled` so no-JS clients still see the copy. Verified: class is in the SSR HTML, removed post-hydration, headline ends at opacity 1, text stays crawlable.
- **Coming Soon anvil: added the ANVL wordmark to the morph cycle + brighter, slightly bigger embers.** Shape order is now `anvil → ANVL crest → ANVL wordmark → The Oath → shirt → barbell → hammer`. `SHAPE_FIT` 3.9 → 4.3 (bigger), `uSize` 0.13 → 0.155 (fuller points), and the fragment shader sits hotter at rest (ember floor 0.2→0.35, stronger additive core). New wordmark samples clean from `/brand/wordmark.svg`; all shaders recompile OK on WebGL2.

## 2026-07-12 — Coming Soon anvil re-forges through brand shapes on strike

- The Coming Soon ember cloud now **morphs shape-to-shape on every hammer strike** (`anvil → ANVL crest → The Oath emblem → compression shirt → barbell → hammer → anvil`), on top of the existing shockwave/heat/camera-recoil. New `src/features/comingSoon/scene/comingSoonShapes.ts` builds one `count × 3` target per shape, all centered + normalized to the shared `SHAPE_FIT` so any two morph 1:1: 3D GLBs (`anvil.glb`, `hammer.glb`) via the area-weighted triangle sampler (extracted here from `EmberAnvil`); the ANVL crest + The Oath marks via SVG-silhouette pixel sampling (`/brand/mark.svg`, `/brand/the-oath-shape.svg` fetched, given explicit px dims, rasterized, alpha-gated); and procedural barbell + compression-shirt solids. 2D marks are laid out flat then given a thin **post-normalization** world-space z-thickness (~0.44 deep) so they stay crisp emblems instead of ballooning into 3D blobs when the fit scale amplifies them.
- **Shader** (`emberForgeShaders.ts`): the target is now `mix(aFrom, aTo, uMorph)` with per-seed stagger (dissolve-and-reform, not a rigid slide) + a mid-morph outward "spark" puff (`sin(uMorph·π)`) that also drives the glow. `EmberAnvil` swaps the two morph-target attributes + tweens `uMorph` 0→1 (GSAP) on `cycleShape()`, wired to the same click as `strike()` in `ComingSoonScene`. GLB targets are ready sync from `useGLTF`; SVG/canvas targets fill in async and `cycleShape` skips any not-yet-loaded slot.
- The idle rotation changed from a **continuous spin to a damped front-facing sway** (`sin(t)·0.16 + pointer.x·0.28`) — a full spin would turn the flat morphed emblems edge-on and unreadable. Verified: `pnpm typecheck`/`build`/coming-soon tests pass; all four new samplers produce NaN-free clouds with correct extents; all four GLSL shaders compile clean on WebGL2.

## 2026-07-11 — Cloudflare plugin restored to standard always-on (post Node 24 upgrade)

- After upgrading the default Node to **24.18.0 LTS**, reverted the temporary command-gated dynamic import of `@cloudflare/vite-plugin` back to the **standard static import, always on** (`vite.config.ts`) bound to the `ssr` vite environment — the official TanStack Start + Cloudflare pattern. `vite dev` now runs SSR on `workerd` too (full dev↔prod parity). All commands now require Node ≥22.15.
- Verified in dev-on-`workerd`: SSR renders (200), `NODE_ENV` correctly resolves to `development` (CSP includes the dev-only `'unsafe-eval'`; CSRF cookie is **not** `Secure` over http — i.e. `wrangler.jsonc` `vars.NODE_ENV=production` does not leak into dev because Vite replaces `process.env.NODE_ENV` per-mode), and the admin session secret resolves (`GET /admin` → 307 redirect to login, no "secret not set" error — the plugin bridges host `process.env`/`.env` in dev, so no `.dev.vars` is required locally). `pnpm typecheck` + `pnpm build` (`✓ built in 2.67s`) pass on the default Node 24.
- Kept the good improvements from the initial setup: `routeFileIgnorePattern`, `server.watch.ignored`, and the per-request CSP (`buildCspReportOnly`).

## 2026-07-11 — Cloudflare Workers deployment setup

- **Prepared the project for production Cloudflare Workers SSR deployment.** Added dev deps `@cloudflare/vite-plugin@^1.44.0` + `wrangler@^4.110.0`; new `wrangler.jsonc` (`name: anvl-athletics`, `compatibility_date 2025-09-02`, `compatibility_flags: ["nodejs_compat"]`, `main: "@tanstack/react-start/server-entry"`, `observability`, `vars.NODE_ENV="production"`); new scripts `deploy` (`pnpm build && wrangler deploy`) and `cf-typegen` (`wrangler types`). Full guide in new `docs/deployment.md`.
- **`vite.config.ts`:** the Cloudflare plugin is loaded via a **command-gated dynamic import** (`command === 'build' || isPreview`) bound to the `ssr` vite environment — so `vite dev` never evaluates the plugin and stays on Node SSR + fast HMR. This matters because `@cloudflare/vite-plugin` (≥1.40) imports `node:module.registerHooks`, which requires **Node ≥22.15**; a static top-level import broke `dev`/`build` on Node 22.14. Dev now works on older Node; only build/preview/deploy need ≥22.15. **Verified `pnpm build` (`✓ built in 4.79s`) + `wrangler deploy --dry-run` (Worker bundles, 202 client assets wired, `env.NODE_ENV` binding confirmed) on Node 24.18.0 LTS.**
- **Env split documented:** `VITE_*` (Supabase URL/publishable key, Shopify Storefront) are build-time-inlined (needed when `vite build` runs, not Worker secrets); `NODE_ENV=production` set in `wrangler.jsonc` vars (Cloudflare doesn't auto-set it — admin/CSRF cookie `Secure` gating + CSP dev relaxations depend on it); `ANVL_ADMIN_SESSION_SECRET` via `wrangler secret put`. Confirmed service-role / Shopify-admin tokens are absent from `src/` (no client leak).
- **`tsconfig.json`:** excluded generated `worker-configuration.d.ts` (its Workers `Element` global collides with the DOM lib — `removeAttribute` returns `Element` vs `void`, which broke a `useEffect` cleanup type); also gitignored (regenerable via `pnpm cf-typegen`). No Worker bindings are used yet.
- **Console cleanup (dev):** `tanstackStart({ router: { routeFileIgnorePattern: 'storefrontMainLayout|__tests__' } })` silences the "does not export a Route" warnings for the co-located helper + specs (→ 0). `server.watch.ignored` now excludes `ds-bundle/`, `.ds-sync/`, `.design-sync/`, `dist-ui/` — Vite was watching the gitignored design-sync artifacts and firing a full page reload per file (the reload storm was also the source of the `Error: aborted (ECONNRESET)`).
- **CSP (`src/start.ts`):** refactored the report-only policy into per-request `buildCspReportOnly(isDev)`. Added `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` (three.js/drei WASM decoders) and `blob:` in `connect-src` (three blob-URL fetches) — prod-safe, prevents the WebGL scenes breaking when the policy switches to enforcing. `'unsafe-eval'` is added **dev-only** (Vite HMR + devtools); production stays strict.
- Node 24.18.0 LTS installed locally via scoop for verification (per-user, no admin); the machine's default `node` (Program Files 22.14) is unchanged — upgrading it (or CI Node) to ≥22.15 is the one remaining step before `pnpm deploy`. Not done: actual `wrangler deploy` (needs Cloudflare login) and GoDaddy → Cloudflare nameserver migration.

## 2026-07-10 — Coming Soon redesign (WebGL ember forge), subscribers panel, GLB upload fix

- **Complete visual redesign** of the reveal page: centered composition, independent of the storefront design language (only theme colors + brand fonts carry over). The centerpiece is a new WebGL "ember forge" (`src/features/comingSoon/scene/`): the bundled `public/about/anvil.glb` is surface-sampled (manual area-weighted triangle sampler — attribute getters, finite-filtered; `MeshSurfaceSampler` chokes silently on some exporter layouts) into 9–24k GPU ember particles that assemble from a nebula on entrance, shimmer, ignite around the cursor, and detonate a radial shockwave on click (camera recoil + `anvl:coming-soon:strike` DOM event → the countdown flares). Ambient drift embers + canvas-texture ground glow; capability-gated (`ComingSoonStage`): WebGL + motion-consent required, touch gets a lite particle budget, reduced-motion keeps the still environment. Countdown digits roll vertically on change. CTA buttons replaced by the email capture (underline input + circular strike button) and **magnetic social icon buttons** (Instagram/TikTok/YouTube/Facebook/email — new `tiktokUrl`/`youtubeUrl`/`facebookUrl` config fields; CTA + foreground-media fields removed). Legibility layer: pointer-transparent dark radial shield between forge and type + text shadows + raised text mixes. Type scale is vh-aware (`clamp(...min(vw,vh)...)`) so one screen fits 375×812 → 1440×900+ with zero scroll.
- **Two hard-won WebGL lessons encoded in the code**: (1) R3F does not preserve the `uniforms` prop object's identity — all runtime uniform mutation must go through the mounted material's ref (this is why the anvil initially never assembled); (2) `gl_PointSize` is hard-capped in-shader and the scatter nebula kept behind the camera plane — near-plane particles otherwise rasterize as screen-sized additive points. Also: TanStack `devtools-vite` source injection (`data-tsd-source`) crashes R3F canvases in dev — every R3F folder is now excluded in `vite.config.ts`.
- **Early-access subscribers panel** in `/admin/coming-soon`: live count, newest-first table, refresh, CSV export (`ComingSoonSubscribersPanel` + `comingSoonSubscribers.service.ts`; RLS already restricted SELECT to admins).
- **GLB upload "invalid MIME" fixed** (`mediaMime.ts` `coerceUploadFile`): supabase-js uploads `File` bodies as multipart `FormData` and silently ignores the `contentType` option in that branch, so a Windows-picked `.glb` (`''`/`application/octet-stream`) hit the bucket's allowed-mime check. Files are now re-wrapped so the Blob itself carries the resolved mime. New tests: `mediaMime.test.ts`, `comingSoonSubscribers.test.ts`.
- Bundled reveal assets renamed to stay self-descriptive in the media library: `public/brand/coming-soon/anvl-coming-soon-{backdrop,ambient,og}.webp`.

## 2026-07-09 — Coming Soon experience + CMS site-mode toggle

- New **Coming Soon site mode**: a CMS-controlled boolean (`coming_soon.enabled`) that replaces every public storefront route with a one-screen cinematic pre-launch reveal while keeping `/admin/*` fully reachable. Gated in `src/routes/__root.tsx`'s `RootLayout` (the same fork that already splits admin from storefront): the flag rides the SSR projection so the first paint is correct, then `useComingSoonConfig` tracks the published row (30s stale + refetch-on-focus) so flipping the toggle updates open tabs without a deploy. No redirects — every gated path serves HTTP 200, and switching off restores the site instantly. Preview bypass for admins: `?anvl-preview=live` arms a sessionStorage flag (`?anvl-preview=off` clears it); while bypassing, a fixed warning ribbon reminds that the mode is still on for visitors. While gated, all public routes except `/` emit `robots: noindex, nofollow`; the home head swaps to the CMS-controlled reveal SEO/OG fields (`src/routes/index.tsx`).
- Storage follows the `shop_config` precedent exactly: new `coming_soon` jsonb on `cms_settings` + `storefront_publication` (migration `20260709120000`, applied via MCP), Zod-validated client-side (`src/features/cms/comingSoon/comingSoon.zod.ts` — flat schema, per-field `.catch` defaults), localStorage working copy `anvl.comingSoon.v1` (`comingSoon.settings.ts`), field-scoped `adminCmsRemoteSync` key, progressive column-fallback select in `publicStorefrontPublication.ts`, and a tolerant separate hydration query in `adminCmsHydration.ts`.
- New `src/features/comingSoon/` (storefront-safe): `ComingSoonExperience` — fixed `inset-0` one-screen layout (no scroll at any viewport; safe-area padded; body copy auto-hides under 640px height), layered `ComingSoonBackdrop` (theme void → backdrop → ambient haze → optional foreground → washes → vignette → grain), crest/wordmark/custom `ComingSoonLogoMark`, timezone-aware `ComingSoonCountdown` (wall-clock + IANA zone → UTC instant via two-pass `Intl` refinement in `lib/countdownTarget.ts`; DST-safe; hides cleanly when disabled), `ComingSoonEmailCapture` (idle/submitting/success/duplicate/error states, honeypot), and `ComingSoonFooterline`. Entrance/parallax in `useComingSoonEntrance`: `gsap.matchMedia` — SplitText headline + staggered reveal ≥768px, `quickTo` pointer parallax ≥1024px fine-pointer, static fade under reduced motion/mobile; `mm.revert()` on unmount. Atmosphere reuses the shared `SiteDustGate`/`DustField` (no new WebGL). The whole experience is `lazy()` from the root, so it costs ~0 while disabled. Page-scoped accent `--cs-accent` (`champagne` #C8A96A or the theme accent) — the global 15-token palette is untouched. All CMS hrefs pass `sanitizeHref`; blank fields fall back to designed defaults in `content/resolveComingSoonContent.ts`.
- New **early-access capture**: `coming_soon_subscribers` table (migration `20260709120001`) — write-only mailbox (anon INSERT-only RLS with basic shape check, admin-role SELECT, no anon read/update/delete, case-insensitive unique email index). `api/subscribeComingSoon.ts` inserts via the existing anon publication client; a unique-violation is surfaced as "already on the list" success.
- New admin editor `/admin/coming-soon` (`src/features/admin/coming-soon/ComingSoonEditor.tsx`, `useSingletonCmsEditor` pattern): master toggle with live badge, copy, countdown (datetime-local + timezone), CTAs, email capture, contact/social, media pickers (backdrop/ambient/foreground/custom logo/OG via `MediaLibraryPickerModal`), accent treatment, SEO/OG fields; rail links the live-preview bypass. Dashboard shows a warning band + Manage link while the mode is enabled; new nav entry in `adminNav.ts`.
- Tests: `comingSoon.zod.test.ts` (defaults/partials/invalid/proto-safety), `countdownTarget.test.ts` (UTC/Beirut summer+winter DST/New York/malformed/unknown-zone/rollover rejection), `resolveComingSoonContent.test.ts` (blank→default, href allowlist, media-index resolution, OG fallbacks, handle validation), `comingSoonGate.test.ts` (exempt paths, lookalike prefixes, preview bypass arm/clear).

## 2026-07-06 — Added storefront global search

- New `src/features/search/` feature: a nav-owned global search covering products, story chapters/acts/cast, About orbs, and per-product PDP editorial copy, plus a hand-curated set of static pages. `lib/searchCorpus.ts` reshapes data already fetched elsewhere (`runtimeClients.commerce.getShopListingCatalog`, `runtimeClients.story.getPublishedChapters`, `loadStorefrontProjection`) into a flat `SearchDocument[]` — no new data-fetching path. `lib/matchEngine.ts` wraps **Fuse.js** (new dependency, ~7kb gzip, own `vendor-fuse` chunk in `vite.config.ts`) for fuzzy ranking + match-range highlighting; it's intentionally index-agnostic (no `runtimeClients`/CMS knowledge) so a future admin CMS search can reuse it with a different document set.
- `useGlobalSearch` (the reusable hook) owns debounced query state (350ms, matching `useShopFilters`'s precedent), a lazily-fetched + 5-minute-cached corpus (`useSearchCorpusQuery`, fetched only on first interaction — never blocking a route's initial load), and per-type navigation (`navigateToResult`).
- UI: `GlobalSearchBar` mounts once in `PremiumNavTopbar` (icon trigger <1024px opening a full-screen overlay directly; inline input + categorized dropdown ≥1024px, with a "See all results" escalation to the same overlay) and once in `PremiumNavMobile`'s drawer (full-width input, `/` shortcut disabled there since the topbar instance owns it site-wide).
- Removed `ShopSearch`'s own `/`-focus keydown binding — the global nav search is now the single canonical `/` target everywhere, including `/shop`, avoiding a double-focus conflict.
- Deep-linking: About orbs and PDP bento tiles (story/materials/care/details) got stable DOM ids + a new shared `useHighlightOnArrival` hook (`src/shared/hooks/`) that scrolls-into-view and pulses (`.anvl-highlight-pulse`, reduced-motion-safe) a section when the URL hash matches it. The Story book overlay (`ChapterBook`) gained an `initialAct` prop so a search hit on a specific act (`?chapter=<slug>&act=<id>`) opens the book straight to that act's spread instead of the cover — the book is paginated, not scrollable, so this is a page-jump rather than a scroll+highlight.
- About's desktop 3D Forge Altar has no per-orb modal deep-link yet, so arriving via an orb search result forces the view mode to the normal scrolling page (logged as a follow-up in `docs/technical-debt.md`).
- New tests: `matchEngine.test.ts`, `searchCorpus.test.ts`, `useGlobalSearch.test.ts`, `useHighlightOnArrival.test.ts`, and a new `ChapterBook.test.tsx` covering the `initialAct` spread-jump logic. Verified end-to-end in a live browser: desktop dropdown with categorized/highlighted results, click-through to a real PDP route, an About-orb hash link scrolling to and highlighting its section, and the mobile icon trigger opening the full-screen overlay.
- **Follow-up fixes** (same day): moved "See all results" to a `sticky top-0` first child of the dropdown so it stays visible while scrolling; added `data-native-cursor` to the dropdown, overlay, and search bar/icon-trigger so The Oath landing page's custom bone cursor (`OathCursor`, which hides the native cursor site-wide via an injected stylesheet) steps aside for a normal cursor over search UI instead of rendering invisible underneath it (the custom cursor's `z-[80]` sits below the dropdown's `z-[85]`/overlay's `z-[95]`); added `data-lenis-prevent` to the dropdown and overlay so Lenis's global wheel interception (which otherwise smooth-scrolls the whole page instead of the nested panel) lets the browser handle wheel scroll natively inside them; and locked `html`/`body` overflow while `GlobalSearchOverlay` is mounted (same pattern as `ChapterBook`) so the storefront behind the full-screen overlay can't scroll.

## 2026-07-06 — Fixed the last-write-wins race between concurrent admin tabs

- **Root cause confirmed:** `adminCmsRemoteSync.ts`'s `flushAdminCmsRemoteSync()` read all 7 `cms_settings`/`storefront_publication` columns fresh from `localStorage` on every save, then blindly wrote **all 7** back in one `UPDATE`, regardless of which single column the current editor actually changed. Two admin tabs saving *different* sections (e.g. Tab A saves Shop while Tab B saves Theme) around the same time could each overwrite the other's column with a stale value, since each tab's in-flight flush captured a snapshot before the other tab's write landed — a classic time-of-check-to-time-of-use race, not something the shared `localStorage` (same-origin, so visible across tabs) fully self-heals on its own.
- **Fix:** threaded an optional `fields: CmsSettingsFieldKey[]` parameter through the whole save chain (`afterLocalCmsMutation` → `flushAdminCmsWriteThrough` → `flushAdminCmsRemoteSync`). Each of the 7 singleton editors' save functions (`saveThemeConfigAsync`, `saveFontConfigAsync`, `saveAssetConfigAsync`, `saveLandingContentSliceAsync`, `saveActiveLandingPageKeyAsync`, `savePdpContentAsync`, `saveShopConfigAsync`) now passes its own specific column name, so its `UPDATE` only ever touches that one column — Postgres leaves every other column alone regardless of what stale value this tab's local snapshot held for it. Omitting `fields` (the debounced auto-sync paths — media index changes, SEO edits, which aren't tied to one of the 7 tracked columns) keeps the previous "sync everything" behavior, since those are lower-frequency and unaffected by this specific race.
- The scoping logic (`pickCmsSettingsFields`) is extracted as a small pure function specifically so it's unit-testable without going through `flushAdminCmsRemoteSync`, which intentionally short-circuits under Vitest (`isTestRunner` check) to guarantee tests never hit real Supabase. New `adminCmsRemoteSync.test.ts` (5 cases) covers the no-scope/single-field/multi-field/exclusion/empty-list behavior.
- `AdminLandingContentEditor`'s combined save (writes both `landing_content` and `asset_config`) already called two separate save functions sequentially — no behavior change needed there beyond each of those two calls now being correctly scoped to its own column.
- `pnpm verify` green (107 files / 646 tests + build).

## 2026-07-06 — Main bundle decomposition: dead end reproduced, real byte-level findings via a workaround

- Regenerated `dist/stats-client.html` fresh and parsed its embedded JSON directly — reproduced the documented gap exactly: the root node is still `server.js`, not the client bundle, confirming this isn't a one-off fluke.
- Instead of forcing the analyzer further, grepped the real `dist/client/assets/index-*.js` (893,699 bytes) for distinctive identifiers each earlier-suspected library would leave post-minification. Concrete findings: `react-hook-form`/`@radix-ui` have **zero** occurrences (ruled out — they're route-code-split, correcting an earlier speculative guess); `@supabase/supabase-js`'s core client (`SupabaseClient`, `GoTrueClient`) is present but its Postgrest/Storage/Functions sub-clients aren't detectable by name (real tree-shaking); `zod` is a confirmed contributor (345 occurrences, `ZodError` spans a contiguous ~51 kB region); GSAP/Three.js/Lenis all show **zero** occurrences (confirms vendor chunking is genuinely clean, no leakage).
- Conclusion: the main chunk is very likely a long tail of many small-to-medium modules (Zod schemas, TanStack Router/Query core, Supabase's core client, shared UI) rather than one removable villain. Full per-module attribution still needs a custom Rollup plugin or a newer TanStack Start bundle-analysis hook — documented as the practical stopping point for this pass, not solved blind.
- `docs/audits/anvl-performance-audit.md` updated with the corrected, evidence-based findings table.

Deliberately minimal: no persistence, no alerting — just enough to see real violations during the report-only trial period before ever considering enforcing mode. Wired into the CSP header via a `report-uri /api/csp-report` directive in `src/start.ts`. Used `report-uri` specifically (not the newer `report-to`/`Reporting-Endpoints` pair) since Safari doesn't support the Reporting API the latter depends on.
- Live-verified end to end on a fresh dev server: the header includes the directive, a POST to the endpoint returns `204`, and the report body is visible in the server log.
- `pnpm verify` green (106 files / 641 tests + build).

## 2026-07-06 — Phase J: security headers, report-only CSP, and CSRF protection implemented

- **New `src/start.ts`** — TanStack Start global request middleware (`createStart`/`createMiddleware`, confirmed via the framework's own bundled skill docs and live-verified with `curl` against a real dev server on an alternate port). Applies to every response — SSR pages, server routes, and server function calls alike:
  - `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains` — safe, no-risk additions.
  - `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` — scoped to the app's actual known origins (Supabase project URL, Shopify Storefront API + CDN, both confirmed live in earlier phases' network traces) rather than a generic template. Shipped in report-only mode deliberately: GSAP/Three.js/Shopify checkout could silently break under a wrong `script-src`/`connect-src`, so this needs a real trial period before ever enforcing.
  - Issues a `anvl_admin_csrf` double-submit-cookie token (plain random value, non-HttpOnly, `SameSite=Lax`) on any request that doesn't already have one.
- **New CSRF protection** (`src/features/admin/auth/adminCsrf.ts`) — a `createMiddleware({ type: 'function' })` wired via `.middleware([csrfProtectionMiddleware])` onto `loginAdminServerFn` and `logoutAdminServerFn` (the only two cookie-authenticated, state-changing server functions in the app — every other admin mutation writes directly to Supabase from the browser with a bearer-token `Authorization` header, which isn't cross-site-forgeable the way an auto-attached cookie is, so it isn't a CSRF target). Client phase echoes the CSRF cookie back as a header; server phase verifies they match via `getRequestHeader`.
  - **Scoped honestly, not oversold:** the session cookie already sets `sameSite: 'lax'` (`adminAuthSession.server.ts`), which already blocks the classic cross-site-POST CSRF vector — this is defense-in-depth on top of that, not the sole protection layer. Documented in the code comment so the actual risk reduction isn't overstated.
  - `verifyCsrfTokens()` extracted as a pure function for direct unit testing; `adminCsrf.test.ts` (10 cases) covers cookie parsing edge cases and the accept/reject matrix.
- Verified live: `curl` against a fresh dev server confirms all headers present on `/`, `/shop`, and `/admin/login` alike (true global middleware, not route-specific).
- `pnpm verify` green (107 files / 646 tests + build).
- Rate limiting (the third Phase J item) is on hold — see `docs/audits/anvl-phase-j-security-plan.md`.

## 2026-07-05 — Full-platform audit (remediation round 2): unsaved-changes warning across all CMS editors

- **New: `/admin` now warns before losing unsaved edits.** Previously flagged as a gap in the CMS audit — no editor warned before navigating away with unsaved changes. Added a shared, minimal-footprint solution rather than a per-editor guard:
  - `src/features/admin/hooks/useAdminDirtyRegistry.ts` — a small Zustand store tracking which editors currently have unsaved edits.
  - `src/features/admin/hooks/useRegisterAdminDirty.ts` — one-line hook each editor calls with its own already-computed `isDirty` boolean; registers on change, clears on unmount.
  - `src/features/admin/components/AdminUnsavedChangesGuard.tsx` — mounted once in `src/routes/admin/route.tsx`, uses TanStack Router's `useBlocker` to intercept in-app navigation (shows a `window.confirm` prompt — no custom resolver, so no new dialog UI to build/maintain) and `enableBeforeUnload` for the browser's own native prompt on tab close/refresh.
  - Wired into all 7 singleton CMS editors (Theme, Shop, Fonts, Assets, PDP, About, Landing Content) — the first 5 via the existing `config`/`stored` JSON-diff pattern (Theme already had this; the rest got the same one-line `useMemo` added), the last 2 (About, Landing Content) via React Hook Form's built-in `form.formState.isDirty`, combined with an asset-config diff for Landing Content since it manages both a form and a separate asset-slot blob.
  - New test file `useAdminDirtyRegistry.test.ts` (8 cases) covers the registry's multi-editor isolation (one editor's unmount doesn't clear another's dirty flag) and the register/cleanup hook's mount/update/unmount lifecycle via `renderHook` — not previously used in this codebase, chosen because the behavior under test (effect-driven register/unregister) isn't observable by calling the store directly.
  - Not live-verified in a real browser this session (no admin credentials available, consistent with every other CMS-editor check in this audit) — `pnpm verify` (105 files / 640 tests + build) is green, and the new tests directly exercise the actual hook/store logic rather than just the pure hint text. Added to the Phase 11 test matrix as a manual QA checklist item for the next person with admin access.
- `docs/audits/anvl-remediation-roadmap.md` updated to mark this item done.
- **New: shared `useSingletonCmsEditor<T>()` hook** de-duplicates the ~15-line read/dirty-track/save/toast lifecycle that Theme, Shop, Fonts, Assets, and PDP Content editors each hand-rolled independently (`src/features/admin/hooks/useSingletonCmsEditor.ts`). Each editor now calls one hook instead of carrying its own `useState`/`useEffect`/`useCallback`/`useRegisterAdminDirty` block; About and Landing Content editors are intentionally left out (React Hook Form–driven, genuinely different shape — forcing them into the same hook would have been the wrong abstraction). 4 new tests (`useSingletonCmsEditor.test.ts`) cover the dirty/clean/save-success/save-failure lifecycle.
  - **Found and fixed a latent test-only infinite-loop trap while writing these tests** — not a production bug, but worth recording: the hook's resync effect (`useEffect(() => setConfig(stored), [stored])`) assumes `stored` is referentially stable across renders unless the underlying data actually changed. A test that recreates `stored` as a fresh object literal inside the `renderHook` callback (rather than via stable `initialProps`) breaks that assumption and loops until Node OOMs. This prompted verifying whether the *real* read functions (`readThemeLibraryFromStorage`, `readFontLibraryFromStorage`, `readAssetConfigFromStorage`, `readShopConfigFromStorage`, `readPdpContentFromStorage`) are actually reference-stable in production — confirmed all 5 are: Theme/Fonts/Assets share a snapshot cache in `cmsSiteConfig.settings.ts`, Shop and PDP each do their own `raw === cachedRaw` check before re-parsing in `shopExperience.settings.ts`/`pdpContent.settings.ts`. No production risk; documented in the new test file's top comment so a future contributor doesn't reintroduce the trap.
- `pnpm verify` green (107 files / 644 tests + build).
- **CLEAN-02 — deleted the dead `MediaPickerField` component** (user-approved). Confirmed via `git grep` at the last commit *before this session touched anything* that every consumer across the whole tree only ever imported its `MediaPickerKind` type — nothing rendered `<MediaPickerField>` as JSX. Root cause: `MediaLibrarySlotField` (what admin editors actually render for slot assignment) has no upload capability of its own — it only picks from the media library, which is populated separately via `/admin/assets`'s `MediaUploadZone`. `MediaPickerField`'s inline-upload UI was superseded by this two-step upload/assign architecture at some point before this audit and never removed. Deleted the component + its test; re-homed the still-needed `MediaPickerKind` type to new `src/features/admin/media/mediaPickerKind.types.ts` and repointed all 6 real (type-only) importers.
  - **Transparency note:** this also revealed that the entire drop-scoped upload module (`uploadCmsMediaFile`, `deleteCmsMediaByPublicUrl`, `formatCmsDropMediaObjectPath` in `uploadCmsMedia.ts`) has zero remaining callers — it was only reachable via `MediaPickerField`, which was *already* dead before this audit started. This means GLB-01's practical, user-facing impact (Phase 0) was smaller than first assumed — the actually-reachable upload path (`MediaUploadZone` → library) already had its fix since 2026-07-03. Both fixes were still correct and worth having; flagging the reachability nuance for honesty rather than overclaiming. The now-fully-dead upload module itself was **not** deleted this pass (new discovery mid-task, outside the specific scope approved) — see `docs/audits/anvl-cleanup-register.md` for the flagged-not-actioned item.
- `pnpm verify` green (106 files / 636 tests + build) after the deletion.
- **SEC-24 fixed (user-approved):** `pg_net` moved out of the `public` schema. Not relocatable (`extrelocatable: false`), so used Supabase's documented `DROP EXTENSION` + `CREATE EXTENSION pg_net SCHEMA extensions` approach instead of a simple `ALTER EXTENSION`. Verified safe first: zero functions anywhere call `net.http_*`, zero rows in `net.http_request_queue`, `cron.job` empty. Security advisor confirms `extension_in_public` cleared.
- **SEC-25 fixed (user-approved):** dropped the public `SELECT` listing policies on both `cms-media` and `story-media` storage buckets. Supabase serves public-bucket object-URL fetches via the bucket's own `public: true` flag, independent of RLS — so the policy was only enabling filename enumeration, not the legitimate fetch-by-URL use case. Verified nothing in the app calls `.storage.from(...).list()` (the media catalog is the `cms_media_assets` table). Live-verified post-fix: a known object's public URL still returns `200`; an anon list call now returns an empty array instead of real filenames.
- `pnpm verify` green (unchanged app code, DB-only migrations); Supabase security advisor re-run shows only `SEC-23` (leaked-password dashboard toggle, not yet actioned) and an informational `touch_row_updated_at` search-path note remaining.

Nothing in this plan has been implemented yet — scoped separately per explicit request, since these are cross-cutting features that needed a deployment decision first, unlike the rest of this session's fixes.

## 2026-07-06 — Phase J: security headers, CSP (report-only), and CSRF implemented

- **Security headers + CSP, applied globally:** new `src/start.ts` — a TanStack Start global request middleware (`createStart({ requestMiddleware: [...] })`) that wraps every SSR page, server route, and server function response. Sets `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Strict-Transport-Security`, and `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` (scoped to `'self'` plus the confirmed-live Supabase and Shopify origins from this audit's own network traces — not enforcing yet, deliberately, since GSAP/Three.js/the storefront's actual script/style needs haven't been verified against a real policy). Live-verified via `curl` across `/`, `/shop`, and `/admin/login` that headers appear correctly and the page still renders.
- **CSRF protection (double-submit cookie):** new `src/features/admin/auth/adminCsrf.ts`. Traced the actual CSRF-relevant surface first rather than assuming — found it's narrower than typically assumed: every CMS editor mutation writes directly browser→Supabase with a bearer token (not cookie-authenticated, not CSRF-forgeable), so the only real target is `loginAdminServerFn`/`logoutAdminServerFn`, the two cookie-authenticated TanStack Start server functions. Also worth noting: the session cookie already sets `sameSite: 'lax'`, which already blocks the classic auto-submitting-form attack — this is defense-in-depth, not the sole layer. Implemented as a function middleware (`createMiddleware({ type: 'function' })`) attached to both functions via `.middleware([csrfProtectionMiddleware])`; the cookie is issued alongside the security headers in `src/start.ts`. 10 new unit tests (`adminCsrf.test.ts`) cover the core double-submit comparison logic directly. Not full-browser-tested end-to-end this session (port conflict with another session's dev server, no admin credentials — consistent with this audit's other admin-UI limitations); flagged as a follow-up manual smoke test in `anvl-phase-j-security-plan.md`.
- Rate limiting remains **not implemented** — blocked on the user setting up an Upstash account (or confirming a different deployment target); see `docs/audits/anvl-phase-j-security-plan.md` §4 for the design.
- `pnpm verify` green (107 files / 646 tests + build) throughout.

## 2026-07-05 — Lighthouse-driven performance fixes: site-wide missing `<title>`, duplicate hero video downloads

Triggered by a Lighthouse report on `/` (Performance 0.43, `document-title` audit scoring 0).

- **PERF-22 — every route on the site was missing a `<title>` element.** `buildSeoMeta()` (`src/app/seo/meta.ts`) returned `{ title, meta, links }` with `title` as a sibling of `meta`, but TanStack Router's `<HeadContent />` only renders `<title>` from a `{ title }` entry **inside** the `meta` array — the top-level field was silently ignored. Every route uses this helper (directly or via `buildSeoMetaFromCmsSource`/`buildSeoHeadForSiteStaticPath`), so this was a site-wide bug, not landing-page-specific. Fixed by adding `{ title }` as the first `meta` array entry; verified `document.title` now resolves correctly on `/`.
- **PERF-23 — the Oath hero video was downloaded 4× (desktop, ~18MB total) / 5× (mobile, ~6MB total) on a single page load.** `preloadUrl()` (`src/features/landingPages/landingEntryLoad.ts`, used by `LandingPageRenderer`'s `LandingPageReadyGate` to warm the entry-overlay progress bar) created a fresh throwaway `<video preload="auto">` element and called `.load()` on every invocation with no de-dupe, plus used `preload="auto"` (full-file download) purely to detect "is this ready" — duplicating what the real `<video>` element in `OathHero` already downloads for playback. Fixed by (1) caching in-flight/completed preloads in a module-level `Map<url, Promise>` so repeat calls never spawn a second shadow element, and (2) switching the shadow video's `preload` to `metadata` (resolving on `loadedmetadata` instead of `loadeddata`/`canplay`), since only duration/reachability needs confirming here — the real hero `<video>` still does the full streaming download. Verified in the network panel: desktop video fetch count 4 → 1, mobile 5 → 2 (one now aborts early instead of re-downloading in full).
- Not fixed this round (flagged, not code bugs): `WarBanner`'s `compression-tee-hero.webp` is a 1085×1335 source displayed at ~161×198 (297KB of the 304KB transferred is wasted) — needs the source asset re-exported at display size or Supabase Storage image transforms enabled (not currently used anywhere in the codebase; risky to wire blind); the root route (`__root.tsx`) and the `/` route (`index.tsx`) each independently call `loadStorefrontProjection()`, fetching the same `storefront_publication` row twice per home-page load — a real TTFB contributor, but deduping loader data across route matches is an architectural change warranting its own discussion rather than a drive-by fix.
- `pnpm typecheck` green; manually verified in preview (title tag, network tab, full accessibility snapshot of the rendered landing page).

## 2026-07-06 — Follow-up pass on the two flagged items above (PERF-24, dedupe retraction)

- **Retracted the "double `loadStorefrontProjection()` fetch" claim from the previous entry.** Reading `fetchPublishedStorefrontProjection()` (`src/features/cms/api/publicStorefrontPublication.ts`) showed it already coalesces concurrent calls in an in-flight `Map<string, Promise<...>>` keyed by Supabase URL+key — `__root.tsx` and `index.tsx` calling it independently was never a double fetch. Verified live: exactly one `storefront_publication` request per home-page load. No code change; this was a correction, not a fix.
- **PERF-24 — shop grid + PDP images requested at full upstream Shopify CDN resolution and downscaled with CSS.** Added `withShopifyImageWidth(src, width)` (`src/shared/lib/url.ts`) — appends Shopify's documented `?width=` CDN query param, no-oping for any URL not served from `cdn.shopify.com` (so seed/local/Supabase-hosted product images pass through unchanged regardless of commerce backend). Applied to the shop grid (`ShopProductCard.tsx`, `ProductCard.tsx` — 800px) and the live PDP gallery (`PdpGallery.tsx` — 1400px main image, 400px thumbnails/mobile carousel; `PdpRelatedCard.tsx` — 400px). `PdpLightbox.tsx` deliberately left untouched — it's the full-size zoom/pan viewer, where full resolution is the point. (First applied to `src/shared/components/ui/ProductGallery.tsx`, which turned out to be dead code never rendered by the live PDP route — re-applied to the actual live components, `PdpGallery.tsx`/`PdpRelatedCard.tsx`, after verifying via network trace that the first attempt produced no visible effect.)
- **`WarBanner`'s oversized `compression-tee-hero.webp` remains unfixed, on hold pending user go-ahead.** Confirmed Supabase Storage image transforms are disabled for this project's plan (`/storage/v1/render/image/public/...` returns `403 FeatureNotEnabled` via direct test) — so there is no code-only fix. The only real fix is re-exporting the source asset at display size and re-uploading it to the live Supabase storage bucket, which is a production content mutation, not a code change.
- Reviewed `/shop`, `/shop/$slug`, `/story`, `/about` route loaders for the same class of problem (missing `Promise.all`, duplicate fetches, unoptimized media) — all already parallelize independent fetches correctly; `StoryMedia.tsx` and `useFittedGltf.ts` already handle image/GLB loading correctly. No further fixes needed on those routes.
- `pnpm typecheck` + `pnpm test` (107 files / 646 tests) green; verified live via network trace that the PDP main image now requests `&width=1400` and thumbnails/related cards request `&width=400`.

## 2026-07-05 — Full-platform audit: remediation round (PERF-20/21, MAINT-02, REU-14)

Continuation of the same-day audit, working down `docs/audits/anvl-remediation-roadmap.md`'s "fix before next deployment" / "performance improvements" / "architecture improvements" sections.

- **PERF-20 (user-approved before applying)**: rewrote 24 RLS policies across `cms_profiles`, `cms_settings`, `landing_pages`, `orders`, `storefront_profiles`, `storefront_publication`, `cms_media_assets`, `story_chapters`/`story_acts`/`story_cast` to wrap `auth.uid()`/`auth.jwt()` in `(select ...)`, so Postgres evaluates them once per query instead of once per row. Every policy's access logic is byte-for-byte identical — pure query-planner fix. Security advisor confirms all `auth_rls_initplan` warnings cleared; no `multiple_permissive_policies` or other warnings introduced.
- **PERF-21**: added the two FK-covering indexes the performance advisor flagged as missing (`cms_media_assets.created_by`, `story_cast.act_id`). Purely additive.
- **MAINT-02**: fully resolved the storefront/shared → `features/admin/**` boundary violation — bigger in scope than first documented. `MediaIndexEntry` (a storefront-relevant type describing `storefront_publication.media_index` entries) was being sourced from `features/admin/media/mediaAssets.types.ts` by **9 files**, including 3 already inside `features/cms/**` reaching backward into admin for their own layer's data. Same for `publicCmsMediaUrl` (a pure, read-only CDN-URL builder with zero admin logic) — sourced from `features/admin/cmsRemote/uploadCmsMedia.ts` by 2 storefront-safe files. Both now live in new `features/cms/media/{mediaIndex.types.ts,mediaUrl.ts}`, with admin re-exporting from there to avoid call-site churn. Separately, `MediaPickerField` — a full React component with its own upload/embed/drag-drop logic — was sitting in `src/shared/components/ui/` despite being imported only by `features/admin/**` (6 files, all for its `MediaPickerKind` type). Moved to `src/features/admin/components/MediaPickerField.tsx` (test file moved alongside, to the project's standard `__tests__` co-location convention). Bonus finding surfaced while tracing every consumer: **nothing in the app actually renders `<MediaPickerField>`** — every real admin editor renders `MediaLibrarySlotField` instead, and only the moved component's own test exercises it; flagged in `anvl-cleanup-register.md` as a high-confidence-but-not-yet-user-confirmed dead-code candidate rather than deleted outright.
- **Bundle impact of MAINT-02**: main client entry chunk dropped 902.02 kB → 890.95 kB raw (274.33 → 271.20 kB gzip) — real but small (~1%), so the fix was worth doing for the architecture violation itself, not as a bundle-size silver bullet. While investigating further, found and partially fixed a `dist/stats.html` tooling bug (`vite.config.ts`'s bundle-analyzer plugin shared one filename across TanStack Start's client and server build passes, so one silently overwrote the other) — split into `dist/stats-client.html`/`dist/stats-server.html` keyed on `isSsrBuild`, but discovered only one file is actually produced and its contents are server-shaped regardless of the flag; full client-bundle decomposition needs a different approach, documented inline in `vite.config.ts` and in `anvl-performance-audit.md` so the dead end isn't rediscovered.
- **REU-14**: migrated all 3 bare-array TanStack Query keys to the `accountQueryKeys`-style factory pattern. Along the way found `ChapterForm.tsx` and `AdminPdpContentEditor.tsx` were each running their own `useQuery` for the **identical** `runtimeClients.commerce.getShopListingCatalog()` call under two unrelated cache keys (`['admin','story-products']` vs `['admin','pdp-products']`) — same data fetched and cached twice. Consolidated onto one new shared hook, `useAdminProductCatalogQuery()` (`features/admin/hooks/`), so both editors now share a single cache entry and network request. `useMediaAssetsQuery.ts`'s single `MEDIA_ASSETS_QUERY_KEY` constant renamed to `mediaAssetsQueryKeys.all` for naming consistency (no behavior change, single consumer).
- `pnpm verify` green throughout (105 files / 632 tests + build at every checkpoint); Supabase advisors re-confirmed clean after the DB migrations.

## 2026-07-05 — Full-platform audit (Phase 3-5): CMS editor review, GLB upload hardening

- **Phase 3 (CMS audit, code-level — no live admin credentials this session):** reviewed save/draft/publish behavior, error handling, and CRUD patterns across the singleton-blob editors (Shop, Theme, Fonts, Assets, About, Content, PDP) plus the relational Story editor. Findings (none fixed yet, tracked for the remediation roadmap): **no unsaved-change navigation warning anywhere in `/admin`** (medium — silent work loss on accidental navigation); **a theoretical last-write-wins race** if two admin tabs edit different fields of the same singleton row concurrently (medium, architecture-inherent, low real-world likelihood); **~40 lines of duplicated read/save/toast boilerplate** repeated per singleton editor (low, reuse candidate); **no role-specific UI gating within editors** (low — mitigated by the route-level `role = admin` gate, defense-in-depth only); **PDP content can orphan** if a product's slug changes after content is authored for it (low, no storefront-visible impact). Story chapter/act/cast deletes are confirmed DB-cascade-safe (`ON DELETE CASCADE`, verified directly against the schema in Phase 4), not just UI-reload-safe as first suspected.
- **Phase 5 (GLB hardening):** added client-side extension + 50MB size validation to `MediaUploadZone.tsx` (`validateUploadFile()`) — the media-library upload path previously relied entirely on the Supabase Storage round-trip to reject bad files, unlike `MediaPickerField`'s asset-slot path which already validated client-side. New test file `MediaUploadZone.test.ts` (5 cases: valid GLB, valid image, unsupported extension, oversized file, exact-boundary file). Also corrected an earlier overly-pessimistic read of MAINT-31 (asset replace orphaning) — `MediaPickerField.applyNextValue()` already deletes the previous Storage object on replace by default; the real gap is narrower (only call sites that opt out via `replaceRemoteAsset={false}`).
- Full GLB upload flow, root cause, and fix documented in `docs/audits/anvl-storage-and-glb-audit.md`; Supabase schema/RLS state documented in `docs/audits/anvl-database-audit.md` and `docs/audits/anvl-security-audit.md`; architecture in `docs/audits/anvl-system-map.md`.
- `pnpm verify` green (105 files / 632 tests + build).

## 2026-07-05 — Full-platform audit (Phase 2): account redirect gate fixed

- **FUNC-01 — `/account` could hang on "Loading your account…" forever for signed-out visitors in a backgrounded tab**: `AccountShellLayout` (`src/features/storefront-account/publicAccount.ui.tsx`) gated its "ready" state (and therefore the redirect-to-sign-in effect) behind a `requestAnimationFrame` callback. Real browsers throttle/suspend `requestAnimationFrame` in hidden/backgrounded tabs by spec — verified directly in this audit (a bare `requestAnimationFrame` probe never fired after 30s+ with `document.hidden === true`). Since `useHydrateStorefrontAccountSession` already hydrates the session synchronously via `useLayoutEffect` (which runs before any regular effect, regardless of tab visibility), the rAF indirection was unnecessary — replaced with a plain `useEffect(() => setReady(true), [])`. Verified: signed-out visit to `/account` now redirects to `/auth/sign-in?redirect=...` within under a second in the same backgrounded-tab preview environment that previously hung. `pnpm verify` green (104 files / 627 tests, no test file existed for this component — gap noted for the Phase 11 test matrix).
- Storefront functional pass (Phase 2 of the ongoing full-platform audit) otherwise came back clean: home/shop/PDP/cart/story/about/404/sign-in/sign-up/forgot-password all rendered with zero console or network errors. One cosmetic nit spotted and fixed: the header cart button's `aria-label` read "Open cart, 1 items" regardless of count — `PremiumNavTopbar.tsx` now pluralizes correctly ("1 item" vs "2 items"). Checkout handoff to Shopify's hosted checkout was verified at the API layer (valid `cartCreate` response, correct `checkoutUrl`, correct `window.location.href` handoff code) but the actual cross-origin navigation couldn't be observed completing in the automated preview browser (confirmed backgrounded/hidden tab) — recommend a real-browser manual smoke test before launch, not currently believed to be a bug.

## 2026-07-05 — Full-platform audit (Phase 0): GLB upload fix, draft-content RLS, dead grant/function cleanup

Kicked off a new whole-platform audit (storefront, CMS, live Supabase project, Shopify, GSAP/Three.js) triggered by a reported `415 invalid_mime_type: application/octet-stream` error uploading `.glb` assets in the CMS. Full plan + findings in the audit conversation; this entry covers the "Phase 0" quick fixes landed immediately. New finding IDs continue the existing scheme from `docs/audit-2026-05-17.md`.

- **GLB-01/GLB-02 — the real root cause of the GLB upload 415**: the 2026-07-03 "GLB mime fix" entry below only fixed `resolveUploadMimeType()` in the **library** upload path (`mediaAssets.service.ts`'s `uploadLibraryMediaFile`). It missed the **drop-scoped / asset-slot** upload path — `uploadCmsMediaFile()` in `src/features/admin/cmsRemote/uploadCmsMedia.ts`, used by `MediaPickerField`'s asset-slot picker (e.g. About page anvil/hammer GLBs) — which still passed raw `contentType: input.file.type || undefined` straight to Supabase Storage. Windows Chrome reports `application/octet-stream` (not empty) for `.glb`, so the `|| undefined` fallback never triggered, and the bucket's mime allowlist (which correctly includes `model/gltf-binary`/`model/gltf+json`, unchanged) rejected it. `registerUploadedCmsMedia()` had the same bug independently for the `cms_media_assets.mime` column. Both now use `resolveUploadMimeType()`.
- **MAINT-30 — de-duplicated the mime/extension/path helpers**: `extensionFor()` was defined verbatim in both `uploadCmsMedia.ts` and `mediaAssets.service.ts`; `formatCmsLibraryMediaObjectPath()` existed in both files with **different, incompatible behavior** (a category-based path in `uploadCmsMedia.ts`, dead code — never imported anywhere — vs. a filename-stem-based path in `mediaAssets.service.ts`, the one actually used). New leaf module `src/features/admin/media/mediaMime.ts` owns `extensionFor` + `resolveUploadMimeType`; both upload paths import from it (no cross-import between the two service files, avoiding a cycle). Dead duplicate path builder deleted.
- **SEC-21 — `cms_settings` (admin draft/working-copy table) was publicly readable**: RLS policy `cms_settings_select_all` had `roles: public, qual: true`, letting anyone query unpublished landing/shop/PDP content via `/rest/v1/cms_settings` before an admin publishes it. Confirmed via grep that no storefront/anon code path reads this table (only `storefront_publication`, the intended anon-safe mirror, and `adminCmsHydration.ts` via the authenticated admin client). Migration `tighten_cms_settings_rls_and_revoke_rls_auto_enable_grant` replaces it with `cms_settings_select_cms`, gated to `cms_profiles.role in (viewer, editor, admin)` — matching every other CMS table's pattern.
- **SEC-22 — stray public EXECUTE grant on `rls_auto_enable()`**: this is an event-trigger helper (`RETURNS event_trigger`) that auto-enables RLS on new tables; it can't do anything useful invoked directly via `/rest/v1/rpc/`, but the security advisor flagged unnecessary `anon`/`authenticated` execute rights. Revoked in the same migration, plus a follow-up (`revoke_public_execute_on_rls_auto_enable`) after discovering the first revoke was a no-op — Postgres's default `GRANT EXECUTE ... TO PUBLIC` at function-creation time is inherited by `anon`/`authenticated` independently of any named-role revoke. Security advisor confirms both `anon_security_definer_function_executable` warnings are now clear.
- **CLEAN-01 — deleted the dead `tm-asset-ingest` Edge Function**: already self-neutralized (`return new Response("gone", {status:410})`, its own comment said "safe to delete from the dashboard"). Removed via `supabase functions delete tm-asset-ingest --project-ref cptebkgyrfmokklwtrgp`.
- Live Supabase inspection (read-only advisors, `list_tables`, `pg_policies`, `execute_sql`) also confirmed as already-compliant/no-action-needed: admin auth (SEC-11), GSAP/Three.js cleanup across `DustField`/`SiteDustGate`/`TheOathLanding`/`AltarAnvil`/Story `Book.tsx`, all admin routes on `lazyRouteComponent`, vendor chunk splitting, and the 8 sampled "maybe unused" dependencies (all genuinely used). Remaining scope (Phase D boundary cleanup, Phase J rate-limiting/CSRF/CSP, PERF-20/21 RLS-initplan + FK-index items, storage bucket listing-policy decision) carried forward, not part of this batch.
- `pnpm verify` green (104 files / 627 tests + build); Supabase security advisor re-run confirms SEC-21/SEC-22 clear with no new issues introduced.

## 2026-07-04 — Care/Size Guide relocated, new legal/FAQ pages, production admin auth

- **Care Guide + Size Guide out of the header topbar**: `navigation.defaults.ts` header links are now Shop/Story/About only. Both guides stay on the footer (now grouped into Shop/Support/Legal columns via `footerLinkGroups`) and were already cross-linked contextually from the PDP (`PdpBuyPanel`'s size selector, `PdpBento`'s Care + Fit&sizing tiles) — no PDP changes needed, that wiring already existed.
- **Two new pages**: `/faq` (accordion covering sizing/shipping/care/orders, cross-linking the other legal/support pages) and `/cookie-policy`, both following the existing `ContentPage` pattern. Footer's new Legal group also surfaces `/privacy`/`/terms`, which existed as routes but had no nav link anywhere — they were previously unreachable from the UI.
- **Admin login is now real, server-validated authentication with Remember Me.** Replaced the dual-mode system (plaintext `VITE_ANVL_ADMIN_PASSWORD` env gate + client-only Supabase bootstrap) entirely:
  - New `src/features/admin/auth/adminAuth.ts` — three TanStack Start `createServerFn`s (`loginAdminServerFn`, `logoutAdminServerFn`, `getAdminSessionServerFn`) run Supabase `signInWithPassword`/`refreshSession` and the `cms_profiles.role = admin` check server-side, using only the anon key (RLS stays enforced, no service-role key involved).
  - New `adminAuthSession.server.ts` — session data (`userId`, `email`, `displayName`, Supabase refresh token, remember-me flag) lives in a sealed, signed, HttpOnly cookie via TanStack Start's `useSession`/`getSession`, keyed by a new server-only `ANVL_ADMIN_SESSION_SECRET`. Every validation call rotates the cookie with Supabase's rotated refresh token — the mechanic that keeps both "remember me" (30-day `Max-Age`) and session-only (no `Max-Age`, dies on browser close) logins alive without ever re-prompting for a password.
  - `src/routes/admin/route.tsx` gained a `beforeLoad` that calls `getAdminSessionServerFn()` and redirects to `/admin/login` when unauthenticated — runs on SSR and on every client-side navigation (via the `/_serverFn` RPC), replacing the old client-only `ProtectedAdminRoute` (deleted, along with `adminAuth.storage.ts` and `adminSupabaseAuthFlow.ts`).
  - `AdminAuthProvider` rewritten as a thin client wrapper: session state comes from the server on mount + a 10-minute heartbeat (keeps the Supabase access token fresh across a long single-page session); `getAdminSupabaseBrowserClient()` (used only for CMS reads/writes, unrelated to the auth gate) now has `autoRefreshToken: false` so the server cookie is the sole refresh-token rotator — letting GoTrue auto-refresh too would race the server for the same one-time-use token.
  - Login form (`-adminLogin.tsx`) simplified to a single Supabase-only flow with a "Remember me for 30 days" checkbox (checked by default).
  - Key gotcha hit during the build: TanStack Start's `import-protection` plugin blocks *any* import of a `**/*.server.*` file from client-reachable code, even a `createServerFn` export — so the file defining the `createServerFn`s had to be plain `adminAuth.ts`, not `adminAuth.server.ts` (only the internal cookie/session helper stays `.server.ts`). Also had to scope the session cookie to `path: '/'`, not `/admin` — server functions are invoked over a shared `/_serverFn/*` RPC route on client navigation, not under `/admin/*`, so a narrower path would have silently dropped the cookie after the first page load.
  - Resolves SEC-01/02/03 and SEC-11 in `docs/technical-debt.md` / `CLAUDE.md`. Not covered (still Phase J): CSRF tokens, CSP/HSTS, rate limiting.
- Verified in-browser: unauthenticated `/admin` redirects to `/admin/login` (confirmed via server log `page_view /admin/login`); a real login attempt against the project's live Supabase round-tripped through `/_serverFn/...` and correctly surfaced "Invalid login credentials" inline. Full success-path (cookie set, CMS pull) not exercised live — no admin credentials available in this session. `pnpm verify` green (typecheck + 104 files / 627 tests + build).

## 2026-07-03 — Story page background visibility

- **`PageBackdrop` gains an `intensity` prop** (`'default' | 'vivid'`): the shared fixed-background component's legibility wash (`color-mix(...,78%,...)`) was drowning the Story page's assigned backdrop photo down to a barely-there texture. `/story` now opts into `intensity="vivid"` (`__root.tsx`) — full-opacity image, a much lighter wash (38% vs 78%), and a lighter top/bottom gradient — while every other route (`/shop`, `/account`, `/auth`) keeps the original default wash untouched.
- Verified: `/story`'s backdrop layer computed to `opacity: 100%` + `38%` wash, `/shop` unchanged at `opacity: 90%` + `78%` wash; `pnpm verify` green.

## 2026-07-03 — GLB mime fix, anvil icon, hero size cleanup, cursor stacking

- **GLB upload 400 fixed**: `resolveUploadMimeType` trusted *any* truthy `file.type`, but Windows Chrome reports the generic `application/octet-stream` for `.glb` (not empty) — so the extension-based inference never ran and the upload hit the bucket's mime allowlist as `application/octet-stream`, which is rejected. Both empty and `application/octet-stream` now count as "unknown" and fall back to inferring `model/gltf-binary`/`model/gltf+json` from the extension. Added regression tests (`mediaAssets.service.test.ts`) covering empty type, octet-stream, a real specific mime, and an unrecognized generic upload.
- **Altar header icon**: swapped the view-switch's "Animated" button from a generic Sparkles icon to lucide's `Anvil`.
- **Story shelves decluttered**: removed the redundant "The Chronicle — Every drop is a chapter…" heading from `ChapterShelf.tsx` and `StoryShelf3D.tsx` — it duplicated the new per-drop Volume Plaque introduced in the Athenaeum redesign.
- **Story + Shop heroes shrunk**: `StoryHero` (padding `py-20/28` → `py-10/14`, heading `clamp(3–7.5rem)` → `clamp(2–4.25rem)`, tightened all internal spacing) and `ShopIntro` (padding `py-14/20` → `py-8/11`, heading `clamp(2.75–6rem)` → `clamp(2–3.5rem)`, plus the compact `heroVisible:false` heading) both now read as intros, not full banners.
- **Custom cursor now paints above the topbar**: `OathCursor` lived inside the landing root's `isolate` stacking context, so its `z-[80]` could never be promoted above a *sibling* of that root (the fixed topbar's cart/avatar, z-40) no matter the number — CSS stacking contexts don't compare across an isolation boundary by raw z-index alone. Portaled to `document.body` (same fix pattern as the story book overlay), so it now compares directly against the topbar and wins. Verified: portal's parent is `document.body`, and a simulated pointer move over the cart control renders the dot/ring at that exact position.
- `pnpm verify` green (105 files / 636 tests).

## 2026-07-03 — About: header + altar/normal switch, forge backdrop, GLB picker polish

- **Small header + view switch** (`AboutHeader.tsx`, `useAboutViewMode.ts`): a compact pill (brand mark + "About") now sits just under the site nav on altar-capable devices (desktop ≥1280px, WebGL, motion allowed), carrying a two-icon toggle — Sparkles for the animated Forge Altar, a rows icon for the classic scrolling page (the same `AboutMobilePage` mobile/tablet already use). The choice persists per browser via `localStorage` and is only ever honoured when the device is actually capable — reload on a phone still always gets the classic page. `AboutAltar`'s orb-picker chip row shifted down to clear the new header.
- **Forge backdrop behind the anvil**: new `forgeBackdrop` asset slot (Page — About) — a Higgsfield-generated blacksmith forge interior with an empty centre floor, bundled as a `public/about/forge-backdrop.webp` default. Painted behind the transparent WebGL canvas; `AltarAurora`'s shader was converted from an opaque plane to a transparent, additive glow (`transparent` + `AdditiveBlending`, alpha-only output) so the aurora shimmers *over* the photo instead of hiding it.
- **GLB upload — confirmed already fully wired, picker polished**: audited the whole path — the `cms-media` Supabase bucket already allows `model/gltf-binary`/`model/gltf+json` at 50 MB, storage RLS already grants editor/admin insert, the upload zone already accepts `.glb`/`.gltf`, and mime inference already covers browsers reporting an empty `file.type` for those extensions. No backend changes were needed. The one real gap: `/admin/assets`' slot-assignment dropdown listed every media library file for every slot regardless of type. `AssetSlotAssignmentPanel` now filters options by the slot's kind (`matchesMediaKind`, exported from `filterMediaLibraryItems.ts`) — a `model` slot only lists GLB/GLTF uploads — while always keeping an already-assigned asset selectable even if it no longer matches, so tightening the filter never orphans an existing valid assignment.
- Verified in-browser (header renders only when capable, toggle switches canvases↔0 and back, forge backdrop image loads with zero failed requests); `pnpm verify` green (105 files / 631 tests).

## 2026-07-03 — Book revert + reworked opening gesture + overlay above the top bar

- **Reverted the previous book "realism" pass** (fore-edge stacks, ribbon, spring settle, velocity flutter): the left stack mesh had a scale bug that floated a paper-thin gilded sheet beside every **closed** book on the shelf. The closed book is back to its original, correct look; page turning is back on the proven settle.
- **Opening redone as one gesture**: the fly-in now uses a decisive `easeOutQuart` flight with a higher lift-off arc and a three-quarter sweep that tips up to face the reader; the cover auto-cracks at ~80% of the flight (750ms, was a sequential 1250ms) so "pull the book to you" and "open it" read as a single motion; the cover swing itself is longer and ceremonial (`easeInOutQuint`, 1.25s) with the settle bounce kept; once open the book **leans in** — easing a breath closer and 5% larger, like a reader bending over the page.
- **Open behavior**: each leaf turn now kicks a small decaying roll through the whole book (it visibly takes the paper's weight) — applied only while the spread is already in motion, so still text never floats.
- **Overlay above the top bar**: `ChapterBook` now portals to `document.body` — it previously rendered inside `main`'s stacking context, so the fixed nav (z-40) painted over the dialog's close button no matter its z-index. Verified live: dialog parent is `<body>`, z 200 vs header z 40, and the X button is hittable at its center point; arrow-key turn and Escape-close confirmed.
- `pnpm verify` green (105 files / 631 tests).

## 2026-07-03 — Story page redesigned as the Athenaeum + realistic book physics + CMS audit

- **Story chrome fully redesigned** (shelf + books untouched): `StoryHero` is now the **Athenaeum frontispiece** — centred gilded typography ("The Forged **Kingdom**" with a foil-gradient accent), a rule·diamond·rule ornament, hall-column hairlines, candlelit ember pools, and an epigraph. `StorySaga` reworked: per-drop shelves now sit under **volume plaques** (Vol. I/II roman numerals + drop label + book count), the empty state matches the archive language, and the closing CTA became a **colophon** (⚒ ornament, small-caps inscription). The reader overlay backdrop gained a candle-pool glow.
- **Open-book realism** (`Book.tsx` + `bookGeometry.ts`): (1) released pages now settle on a **damped spring that inherits the hand's release momentum** (`SETTLE_SPRING_K/DAMP`) instead of an exponential glide; (2) drag/flick **velocity feeds the paper's curl amplitude** — hard turns visibly whip the sheet; (3) **paper mass redistributes**: the gilded fore-edge stacks thin on the right and grow on the left as you read (tops stay fixed — the DOM page layer's alignment is untouched); (4) a **ribbon bookmark** in the chapter's CMS foil colour drapes down the right page with its tail past the bottom edge.
- **CMS wiring audit (About + Story)**: Story is fully sound — remote Supabase columns match the repo migrations and row mapper exactly (chapters/acts/cast incl. product/drop linkage), `story-media` + `cms-media` buckets exist and are public, RLS policies verified, and the admin editor covers every schema field (chapter sort order intentionally rides `chapterNumber`). About fixes: stale v2-era slot/marquee hints corrected, and `hero.scrollCue` (editable but previously unrendered) now shows on the mobile hero.
